The combination of rotating hours, middle-of-the-night calls, and emotionally intense end-of-life care gradually eroded his usual structure of hunting weekends, line-dancing nights, and family barbecues that had kept him grounded. Over several months, his family and coworkers noticed that he became increasingly preoccupied with unusual ideas about his special role in protecting his clients and community, and he began to clash with supervisors as his decisions at work and church grew inconsistent with safety protocols and expectations. The functional strain—missed documentation, erratic behavior during safety briefings, and concerns raised by his pastor and wife—finally led his agency to insist on an occupational health evaluation, at which point he was referred for psychiatric assessment.\n- Chief Complaint: “I feel like God’s picked me out personally to fix everything in this town, and nobody understands how important my mission is.”\n▸ Patient-specific Symptom Manifestations\n- S019 (Delusions): Even when my wife shows me the bank statements and says the agency is just short-staffed, I still know the higher‑ups and maybe even the county are watching me because they think I’m the only one who can keep these seniors safe, and they’re trying to trip me up if I don’t follow what God’s telling me instead of their rules.\n- S020 (Hallucinations): A lot of nights at work or even in my own living room, I hear this deep, calm voice that isn’t mine telling me what to do—like which client to visit first or when to pray over somebody—and sometimes I hear people at church whispering my name and saying I’m chosen, even when they swear they never said anything.\n- S022 (Disorganized_Abnormal_Behavior): My wife and coworkers tell me I’ll suddenly start pacing fast up and down the hallway, praying out loud with my hands in the air or giving these long, serious speeches to nobody in particular in the break room, right in the middle of a shift when I’m supposed to be doing charting or helping a client to the bathroom.\n- S023 (Negative_Symptoms): Other times I just go flat and empty, sitting in my recliner or in the break room staring at the wall for an hour, not answering folks much more than a word or two and not caring about hunting, dancing, or even playing with my kids like I used to.\n- S033 (Elevated_Irritable_Mood): I swing from feeling on top of the world and super fired up—like I’m full of electricity and everyone should just get out of my way—to snapping at my supervisor or my pastor over the smallest thing because I’m sure they’re blocking my mission, and my mood can flip like that several times in the same day.\n- S034 (Increased_Activity): When I’m in that high gear, I can’t sit still at all—I’ll clean the whole house at 3 a.m., rewrite care plans for clients who aren’t even mine, start organizing new programs at church, and keep bouncing from one thing to the next without ever really finishing anything.\n- S035 (Inflated_Self_Esteem): For a long time now I’ve honestly believed God singled me out to be some kind of protector for the whole town, like I can see dangers and truths other people can’t, so I don’t feel like the normal rules at work or church should apply to me when they get in the way of what I’m supposed to do.\n- S036 (Decreased_Sleep): I’ll go on three or four hours of sleep for several nights in a row, answering calls, planning projects, and reading the Bible or medical stuff online, and instead of feeling tired I just feel revved up, like sleep is a waste of time when there’s so much I’m meant to be doing.\n- S038 (Racing_Thoughts): My mind races so fast that by the time I’m halfway through telling my wife one idea about starting a new ministry for the seniors, I’ve already jumped in my head to five other plans about fixing the agency, the church, and the whole town, and I can’t slow it down enough to stick with one thought.\n- S039 (Manic_Distractibility): When I try to focus on something simple like filling out a medication log, the slightest noise—a TV in another room, a car driving by, a bird at the window—or even a random thought about a Bible verse will pull me away, and I find myself halfway across the building doing something else before I remember what I was supposed to be doing.\n▸ Additional Diagnostic Requirements\n- psychotic_symptoms and manic_episode occur concurrently: When I’m running on almost no sleep, buzzing with energy and convinced I’ve got to overhaul the whole agency and church, that same deep voice is in my ear telling me exactly what order to do things in and I hear people whispering my name like I’m chosen, all happening at the same time.\n- manic_episode is present for the majority of the total duration of the active and residual portions of the illness: Looking back over this whole stretch since I took on the heavier caseload, I’ve spent way more time feeling wired, overly driven, and full of big missions than in those flatter spells where my energy drops off.\n- delusions_and_hallucinations occur for 2 or more weeks in the absence of a major mood episode: Even during times when my mood has felt more just ‘blah’ and low-key instead of fired up, I’ve still been sure that the county and my bosses were tracking me as God’s special protector and I kept hearing that calm voice giving me instructions for weeks at a time.\n- functional_impairment: My boss cut my hours and put me on probation because of missed charting and unsafe decisions, my pastor asked me to step back from leading anything at church, and at home my wife says I’m so wrapped up in this mission that I barely help with the kids or bills anymore.\n- duration: This has been building ever since I agreed to take on the extra complex clients and rotating shifts, which was a little over a year ago and has just kept getting worse.\n- psychotic_symptoms_duration: The clear sense of being watched and chosen, along with hearing that guiding voice, has been going on steadily for most of this past year, especially over the last several months.\n- manic_episode_duration: I’ve been in that revved-up, can’t-sit-still, on-a-mission mode for most of the time since about a month or two after my workload jumped, and it really hasn’t let up much since then.\n\nRules:\n- If thought.\n- S039 (Manic_Distractibility): When I try to focus on something simple like filling out a medication log, the slightest noise—a TV in another room, a car driving by, a bird at the window—or even a random thought about a Bible verse will pull me away, and I find myself halfway across the building doing something else before I remember what I was supposed to be doing.\n▸ Additional Diagnostic Requirements\n- psychotic_symptoms and manic_episode occur concurrently: When I’m running on almost no sleep, buzzing with energy and convinced I’ve got to overhaul the whole agency and church, that same deep voice is in my ear telling me exactly what order to do things in and I hear people whispering my name like I’m chosen, all happening at the same time.\n- manic_episode is episode: Even during times when my mood has felt more just ‘blah’ and low-key instead of fired up, I’ve still been sure that the county and my bosses were tracking me as God’s special protector and I kept hearing that calm voice giving me instructions for weeks at a time.\n- functional_impairment: My boss cut my hours and put me on probation because of missed charting and unsafe decisions, my pastor asked me to step back from leading anything at church, and at home my wife says I’m so wrapped up in this mission that I barely help with the kids or bills anymore.\n- duration: This has been building ever since I agreed to take on the extra complex clients and rotating shifts, which was a little over a year ago and has just kept getting worse.\n- psychotic_symptoms_duration: The clear sense of being watched and chosen, along with hearing that guiding voice, has been going on steadily for most of this past year, especially over the last several months.\n- manic_episode_duration: go on three or four hours of sleep for several nights in a row, answering calls, planning projects, and reading the Bible or medical stuff online, and instead of feeling tired I just feel revved up, Subtypes: Self-Induced Vomiting, Misuse of Medications\n[S049] Eating_Large_Amount: Eating, in a discrete period of time, an amount of food that is definitely larger than what most individuals would eat in a similar period of time under similar circumstances.\n[S050] Loss_of_Control: A sense of lack of control over eating during the episode (e.g., a feeling that one cannot stop eating or control what or how much one is eating).\n[S051] Rapid_Eating: Eating much more rapidly than normal.\n[S052] Eating_Until_Uncomfortably_Full: Eating until feeling uncomfortably full.\n[S053] Eating_Without_Hunger: Eating large amounts of food when not feeling physically hungry.\n[S054] Eating_Alone_Due_to_Embarrassment: Eating alone because of feeling embarrassed by how much one is eating.\n[S055] Negative_Affect_After_Binge: Feeling disgusted with oneself, depressed, or very guilty afterward.\n[S084] Low_Weight_Severity_Non_Recognition: Persistent lack of recognition of the seriousness of the current low body weight.  Subtypes: Denial of Medical Risk, Disregard of Symptoms, Dismissal of Concerns\n[S056] Excessive_Anxiety_Worry: Excessive anxiety and worry (expectant apprehension) occurring persistently about a number of events or activities.  Subtypes: Chronic Worry, Generalized Worry, Catastrophizing, Intolerance of Uncertainty\n[S057] Uncontrollable_Worry: The subjective experience of finding it difficult to stop or control the worry.  Subtypes: Failure to Control, Intrusive Worry, Chaining of Worry\n[S058] Restlessness_Tension: Restlessness, or feeling keyed up or on edge.  Subtypes: Feeling Keyed Up, Physical Tension, Nervousness\n[S059] Concentration_Difficulty: Difficulty concentrating or mind going blank.  Subtypes: Interference by Worry, Inability to Sustain Attention, Mind Going Blank\n[S060] Irritability: A state of reacting sensitively or sharply to minor stimuli.  Subtypes: Low Frustration Tolerance, Snappiness, Exhausted Patience\n[S061] Muscle_Tension: A state where muscles are continuously tense, stiff, and accompanied by discomfort.  Subtypes: Neck and Shoulder Tension, Jaw Tension, Generalized Muscle Aches, Chronic Stiffness\n[S062] Specific_Phobic_Fear: Marked fear or anxiety about a specific object or situation that is out of proportion to the actual danger. Subtypes: Animal Type, Natural Environment Type, Blood-Injection-Injury Type, Situational Type, Other Type\n[S063] Immediate_Fear/Anxiety_Response: The phobic object or situation almost always provokes immediate fear or anxiety.  Subtypes: Anticipatory Anxiety, Immediate Panic Response, Physiological Panic Symptoms, Freezing\n[S064] Phobic_Avoidance_Endure: The phobic object or situation is actively avoided or endured with intense fear or anxiety. Subtypes: Contamination Obsessions, Pathological Doubt, Symmetry and Exactness Obsessions, Forbidden or Taboo Thoughts, Somatic Obsessions\n[S066] Compulsions: Repetitive behaviors or mental acts performed in response to an obsession or to prevent anxiety/distress; clearly excessive or not realistically connected.  Subtypes: Cleaning or Washing Compulsions, Checking Compulsions, Ordering and Arranging Compulsions, Mental Acts, Repeating Rituals\n[S067] Preoccupation_with_Appearance: Preoccupation with one or more perceived defects or flaws in physical appearance that are not observable or appear slight to others.  Subtypes: Skin Defects, Facial Features, Asymmetry, Muscle Dysmorphia, Specific Body Parts\n[S068] Appearance_Related_Repetitive_Behaviors: Repetitive behaviors or mental acts performed in response to appearance concerns.  Subtypes: Mirror Checking or Avoidance, Excessive Camouflaging, Skin Picking, Reassurance Seeking, Comparing, Seeking Cosmetic Procedures\n[S069] Intrusive_Symptoms: Recurrent, involuntary, and intrusive distressing memories of the traumatic event.  Subtypes: Intrusive Memories, Distressing Dreams, Dissociative Reactions (Flashbacks), Psychological Distress at Cues, Physiological Reactions to Cues\n[S070] Trauma_Avoidance: Persistent avoidance of stimuli associated with the traumatic event.  Subtypes: Internal Avoidance, External Avoidance\n[S071] Trauma_Related_Amnesia: Inability to remember an important aspect of the traumatic event (typically due to dissociative amnesia and not to other factors like head injury or alcohol).  Subtypes: Fragmented Memory, Generalized Amnesia\n[S072] Negative_Beliefs: Persistent and exaggerated negative beliefs or expectations about oneself, others, or the world.  Subtypes: Negative Self-Beliefs, Negative Beliefs about Others, Negative Worldview\n[S073] Distorted_Cognitions_With_Blame: Persistent, distorted cognitions about the cause or consequences of the traumatic event that lead the individual to blame himself/herself or others.  Subtypes: Self-Blame, Blaming Others\n[S074] Persistent_Negative_Emotional_State: Persistent negative emotional state (e.g., fear, horror, anger, guilt, or shame).  Subtypes: Persistent Fear, Persistent Horror, Persistent Anger, Persistent Guilt, Persistent Shame\n[S075] Social_Detachment: Feelings of detachment or estrangement from others.  Subtypes: Emotional Detachment, Social Isolation, Difficulty with Intimacy, Loss of Belonging\n[S076] Inability_Positive_Emotions: Persistent inability to experience positive emotions (e.g., inability to experience happiness, satisfaction, or loving feelings).  Subtypes: Anhedonia for Happiness, Lack of Satisfaction, Inability to Feel Love, Emotional Numbness\n[S077] Irritability_Aggression: Irritable behavior and angry outbursts (with little or no provocation) typically expressed as verbal or physical aggression.  Subtypes: Impulsive Anger, Verbal Aggression, Physical Aggression, Chronic Irritability\n[S078] Reckless_Self_Destructive_Behavior: Reckless or self-destructive behavior pattern that endangers one's safety or health.  Subtypes: Risky Behaviors, Substance Abuse, Indisicriminate Sexual Behavior, Self-Harm\n[S079] Hypervigilance: Persistent state of being alert and tense about the surrounding environment.  Subtypes: Scanning for Danger, Safety Checking, Sustained Alertness\n[S080] Exaggerated_Startle_Response: Exaggerated startle response to minor stimuli.  Subtypes: Auditory Startle, Tactile Startle, Visual Startle, Slow Recovery\n[S081] Stress_Related_Maladaptive_Response: Emotional or behavioral symptoms appearing in response to an identifiable stressor.  Subtypes: With Depressed Mood, With Anxiety, With Mixed Anxiety and Depressed Mood, With Disturbance of Conduct, With Mixed Disturbance of Emotions and Conduct, Unspecified\n[S082] Depersonalization_Derealization: Altered sense of the reality of one's self or surroundings after a traumatic event.  Subtypes: Depersonalization, Derealization, Time Distortion\n\n=== PATIENT UTTERANCE ===\nEven during times when my mood is flat or just \"blah\" instead of fired up, I still hear that calm voice giving me instructions and know I'm God's chosen protector. The feeling that my bosses and the county are tracking me stays with me for weeks at a time, even without the high energy.\n\nTask:\n- CONFIRMED: symptoms clearly present or strongly implied by the patient's words.\n- DENIED: symptoms the patient explicitly says they do NOT have\n  (e.g. \"No\", \"I don't\", \"I haven't\", \"never\").\n- Do NOT speculate.
